The setting is RLHF (and similar constrained-training regimes): a true predictor $f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}}$ in a function space, a constraint that pulls the trained predictor away from truth, queries that read out the trained predictor along chosen directions. In the linearized neighborhood \\\\\\\\u2014 where the constraint manifold is approximated by its tangent space at the operating point \\\\\\\\u2014 training reduces to projection $f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{trained}} = P_C f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}}$, query readout reduces to projection $P_Q$, and the resulting phantom $P_Q(I - P_C)\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\,f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}}$ has the operator-theoretic anatomy of Halmos two-projection geometry.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n**The scope of this document is exactly the linearized neighborhood and no further.** Within that scope:\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n- The four-object decomposition (retention $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\beta^2$, suppression $(1-\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\beta)^2$, leakage $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\beta(1-\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\beta)$, total error $1-\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\beta$) gives exact identities for the components of phantom magnitude as functions of principal-angle geometry.\\\\\\\\n- The three-flavor classification (first-moment, second-moment, eigenstructure phantoms) characterizes how phantom content propagates through downstream pipelines.\\\\\\\\n- Predictions about which queries are most distorted, how distortion compounds across pipelines, and what disconfirmers would falsify the framework are derived under the linearization assumption.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe Halmos formulation is what makes the document tractable. Two non-commuting orthogonal projections in a Hilbert space have a closed-form decomposition (CS, principal angles, canonical pairs); the four-object scalar laws fall out of the algebra; the three-flavor classification follows from how the algebra propagates through downstream pipelines. None of this requires nonlinear-manifold machinery. **The linearization is the price of admission to the operator-theoretic toolkit, and the document pays it explicitly.**\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nWhat lives outside this document. The linearization is valid in the weak-constraint / small-deviation regime; **strong-constraint regimes** (where the trained policy is far from the reference in KL, where the constraint manifold's curvature matters, where the e-projection's exponential tilt is not well-approximated by linear projection) require nonlinear-manifold treatment in the appropriate Riemannian (Fisher / KL) geometry. That treatment lives in the companion `info_geometric_reformulation.md`, which carries out the parallel KL-geometric development including:\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n- The exact e-projection identity for RLHF: $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{trained}} \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\propto \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{ref}}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\, e^{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\lambda^* r}$, with the closed form holding without linearization.\\\\\\\\n- The Bregman triangle decomposition with explicit cross-term $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\lambda^*(R_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}} - R_0)$ that the linearized Pythagorean misses.\\\\\\\\n- The four-object decomposition's RLHF-setup analog (Bridge B), at first order in the Lagrange multiplier with an explicit second-order curvature term identified.\\\\\\\\n- The numerical observation that aligned filter+constraint is *least*-compounding (211/240 cases pointwise, mean and median across the test family) \\\\\\\\u2014 a result that contradicts the linearized framework's \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"compounding\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\" intuition once the curvature is properly accounted for.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThis document and the reformulation companion together cover linearized and nonlinear regimes; they should be read as complementary scope-restricted treatments of the same phenomenology. **The linearization developed here is the natural entry point to the nonlinear theory** \\\\\\\\u2014 the principal-angle geometry, the four-object decomposition, the three flavors, and the phantom predictions are easier to build intuition for in the Hilbert setting and then to recognize (with appropriate corrections) in the curved-manifold setting.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nWhat this document does not do. It does not derive the constraint subspace from first principles, characterize specific training pipelines empirically, or propose new training methods. It formalizes what the operator framework predicts about the shape of distortion in the linearized regime, and identifies which predictions are testable.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n---\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n## Part I \\\\\\\\u2014 Setup\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n### 1.1 The Function Space (Linearized Setting)\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nLet $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}$ be the input space (e.g., token sequences) and $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{Y}$ the output space (e.g., next-token distributions, or reward-relevant readouts). Let $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu$ be a base measure on $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}$ \\\\\\\\u2014 for a language model, the natural choice is the data-generating distribution.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n**Two natural Hilbert-space realizations** depending on what kind of predictor is being analyzed:\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nFor **regression-type readouts** where $f(x) \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\in \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{R}^d$ directly (real-valued logits, embedding-space coordinates, or any deterministic readout), the natural function space is\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n$$H = L^2(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu; \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{R}^d), \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\qquad \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\langle f, g\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\rangle = \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\int_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}} f(x) \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\cdot g(x)\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\, d\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu(x).$$\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThis is the cleanest Hilbert-space realization and is the setting in which Halmos two-projection geometry applies directly without further approximation.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nFor **distributional outputs** (next-token policies $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\cdot|x)$ on a discrete vocabulary), the natural ambient space is the manifold of conditional distributions with KL/Fisher Riemannian structure \\\\\\\\u2014 a curved manifold, not a Hilbert space. Halmos two-projection geometry does not apply on this manifold globally. **What does apply, locally, is the Fisher-tangent linearization at a chosen base point.** Identify a policy $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi$ near a base $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0$ (typically $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{ref}}$ or $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{trained}}$) with its score-function representation $u = \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\log\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi - \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\log\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0$ in the tangent space $T_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{P}$, equipped with the Fisher inner product\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n$$\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\langle u, v\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\rangle_F = \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{E}_{x \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\sim \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\,\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{E}_{y \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\sim \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\cdot|x)}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\,[u(x,y)\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\,v(x,y)].$$\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nIn this tangent Hilbert space, the linear-algebra apparatus of Halmos applies, and the constraint manifold is approximated by its Fisher-tangent space at the base point.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n**This document operates in the Hilbert-space setting throughout.** Where \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"$H$\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\" appears below, it is either the regression-case $L^2$ space or the Fisher-tangent space at the relevant base point; both are Hilbert spaces and the operator-theoretic results are identical in form. Predictions about phantom magnitude, principal-angle geometry, and the four-object decomposition are exact in the tangent space; their accuracy as descriptions of actual policies depends on how far the operating point sits from the linearization base. The leading correction is the second-order term $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\frac{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\lambda^2}{2}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\tilde r^2 - \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{E}_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0}[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\tilde r^2])$ in the e-projection expansion (companion `info_geometric_reformulation.md` \\\\\\\\u00a7B.5), which bends the trained policy off the linear constraint direction at order $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\lambda^2$ in the constraint strength.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe two-tier setup \\\\\\\\u2014 regression case in $L^2$, distributional case in Fisher tangent \\\\\\\\u2014 exists because real RLHF works on distributional outputs but the Halmos toolkit needs Hilbert structure. Choosing the Fisher tangent as the linearization base point converts the curved problem into a flat one locally and lets the projection framework apply. The trade-off is explicit in the regime-of-validity: predictions in this document hold to leading order in the constraint strength; the strong-constraint regime is the companion document's territory.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n### 1.2 The True and Trained Predictors\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe **true predictor** $f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}} \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\in H$ is the optimal predictor under the data-generating distribution \\\\\\\\u2014 the conditional expectation $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{E}_\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{Y} \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mid \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}]$ for regression-type readouts, or the conditional log-density for distributional outputs. RLHF rewards certain outputs and penalizes others. Hallucination suppression penalizes confident assertions in regions of the data manifold where the training signal is uncertain. Ideological filters force conclusions in particular categories of input.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nDefine the **constraint subspace** $C \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\subset H$ as the closed subspace of predictors consistent with all the constraints jointly. A predictor $f \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\in C$ satisfies every constraint (in the linearized sense \\\\\\\\u2014 produces zero penalty under the reward model, zero forced override, zero excursion-penalty signal). The orthogonal complement $C^\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\perp$ is the subspace of predictors that violate at least one constraint.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nIn actual training $C$ is defined implicitly by a reward model $r: H \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\to \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{R}$ via $C = \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\{f : r(f) \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\geq r_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\max} - \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\epsilon\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\}$ for some tolerance $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\epsilon$, which is generally a manifold rather than a linear subspace. The linearization replaces the manifold with its tangent space at $f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{trained}}$, which is a subspace and admits an orthogonal projection $P_C$. The framework operates in this linearized neighborhood. Nonlinear corrections are flagged but not developed here.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n### 1.4 Training as Projection (Within the Linearization)\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nWithin the Hilbert-space setting of \\\\\\\\u00a71.1 (regression case directly, distributional case via Fisher-tangent linearization at $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0$):\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n$$f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{trained}} = P_C f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}},$$\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nwhere $P_C$ is orthogonal projection onto the (linearized) constraint subspace $C$. The spectral framework's machinery applies when $I_G$ is approximately linear in the local representational neighborhood of the trained model. For transformer architectures this approximation is reasonable for queries close to $\\\\\\\\partial G$ in the architecture's feature metric; it degrades for queries deep inside $G$.\\\\n    22\\\\t\\\\n    23\\\\tThe scope of this document is exactly the regime where both linearizations hold: distributional-output predictions in the Fisher tangent at a base point, and interpolation-operator predictions in the local feature-metric neighborhood of $\\\\\\\\partial G$. Within that regime, the four-object scalar decomposition is exact, the bulk-edge-projector anatomy applies, and the T6 analog has a precise (though architecture-specific) form.\\\\n    24\\\\t\\\\n    25\\\\tWhat lives outside this document. The strong-deviation regime \\\\u2014 where the trained policy is far from the reference, or where the query sits deep inside $G$ relative to the architecture's effective length scale \\\\u2014 requires nonlinear-manifold treatment. The companion `info_geometric_reformulation.md` carries out the parallel KL-geometric development for the constraint side; the analogous nonlinear treatment for the interpolation side (architecture-specific, with the second-order corrections to $I_G$ propagated through) is open work. Earlier drafts of this document (and the trilogy's framing more generally) claimed that filter and constraint compound rather than cancel because they are shaped by the same content goals. **The companion's verified numerical work shows the opposite holds in the global KL geometry: aligned filter+constraint configurations produce the smallest total error in 211 of 240 cases (mean and median across the test family).** Section 5.2 has been rewritten accordingly. The compounding intuition was a linearized-regime artifact; the corrected statement is that aligned configurations are typically least-compounding, with anti-aligned configurations producing the strongest compounding effects. This corrects the most consequential single claim of earlier drafts.\\\\n    28\\\\t\\\\n    29\\\\tWhat this document does not do. The framework does not characterize the architecture's inductive bias from first principles, does not prescribe what filters do or do not damage which query distributions, does not predict specific outputs of specific systems. It identifies what kind of distortion data holes geometrically force in the linearized regime and how that distortion combines with constraint distortion when both are applied, with the corrected combining rule sourced from the companion document.\\\\n    30\\\\t\\\\n    31\\\\t---\\\\n    32\\\\t\\\\n    33\\\\t## Part I \\\\u2014 Setup\\\\n    34\\\\t\\\\n    35\\\\t### 1.1 The Input Space and the Data Distribution\\\\n    36\\\\t\\\\n    37\\\\tLet $\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}$ be the input space (token sequences for language models, latent codes for image generators, or whatever the model's input domain is). Let $\\\\\\\\nu$ be the data-generating distribution on $\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}$. Let $f_{\\\\\\\\text{true}}: \\\\\\\\mathcal{X} \\\\\\\\to \\\\\\\\mathcal{Y}$ be the truth \\\\u2014 the ideal predictor the data are sampled from, in the regression-type framing, or the conditional log-density in the distributional framing.\\\\n    38\\\\t\\\\n    39\\\\tA **data hole** is a region $G \\\\\\\\subset \\\\\\\\mathcal{X}$ where the training corpus contains no samples from $\\\\\\\\nu$. The hole may arise from genuine data absence (gaps in observation, undersampled regions) or from purposeful exclusion (curation, content filtering, demographic-balance sampling). The operator-theoretic structure does not distinguish these \\\\u2014 what matters is that the training signal in $G$ is zero.\\\\n    40\\\\t\\\\n    41\\\\tThe **filtered distribution** seen by the training process is $\\\\\\\\nu \\\\\\\\cdot \\\\\\\\mathbb{1}_{\\\\\\\\Omega \\\\\\\\setminus G}$, where $\\\\\\\\Omega = \\\\\\\\mathcal{X}$ is the full input space. The data the model fits is samples from this filtered distribution, not from $\\\\\\\\nu$.\\\\n    42\\\\t\\\\n    43\\\\t### 1.2 The Trained Predictor\\\\n    44\\\\t\\\\n    45\\\\tLet $\\\\\\\\hat f$ denote the predictor the training process converges to. In the well-specified-model limit (architecture can represent $f_{\\\\\\\\text{true}}$, infinite capacity, infinite data on $\\\\\\\\Omega \\\\\\\\setminus G$), the trained predictor satisfies:\\\\n    46\\\\t\\\\n    47\\\\t$$\\\\\\\\hat f|_{\\\\\\\\Omega \\\\\\\\setminus G} = f_{\\\\\\\\text{true}}|_{\\\\\\\\Omega \\\\\\\\setminus G}$$\\\\n    48\\\\t\\\\n    49\\\\t\\\\u2014 the trained predictor agrees with the truth on the support of the filtered distribution.\\\\n    50\\\\t\\\\n    51\\\\tOn $G$, the trained predictor is not determined by the data. It is determined by the architecture's inductive bias plus whatever boundary information from $\\\\\\\\Omega \\\\\\\\setminus G$ the architecture propagates. Write:\\\\n    52\\\\t\\\\n    53\\\\t$$\\\\\\\\hat f|_G = I_G(\\\\\\\\hat f|_{\\\\\\\\partial G})$$\\\\n    54\\\\t\\\\n    55\\\\twhere $I_G$ is the **interpolation operator** of the architecture \\\\u2014 the map from boundary information at $\\\\\\\\partial G$ to the function value inside the hole. This is the analog of the Green's function for an elliptic operator: a way of extending boundary data into the interior. Different architectures have different $I_G$. Transformer architectures have $I_G$ that is approximately linear in the local representational neighborhood plus nonlinear corrections; classical kernel methods have $I_G$ that is exactly the kernel-weighted extension; Gaussian processes have $I_G$ that is the conditional-mean extension.\\\\n    56\\\\t\\\\n    57\\\\t**Linearization assumption.** The operator-theoretic spine of this document treats $I_G$ as approximately linear in the local representational neighborhood of $\\\\\\\\partial G$. For Gaussian processes and kernel methods this is exact. For transformer architectures it holds for queries close to $\\\\\\\\partial G$ in the architecture's feature metric; queries deep inside $G$ relative to the architecture's effective length scale (\\\\u00a73.2) lie outside this approximation and require nonlinear treatment that this document does not provide. The regime of validity for the predictions below is the boundary-layer neighborhood \\\\u2014 close enough to $\\\\\\\\partial G$ that the architecture's interpolation is well-approximated by its linear part.\\\\n    58\\\\t\\\\n    59\\\\tThe full trained predictor in the linearized regime is:\\\\n    60\\\\t\\\\n    61\\\\t$$\\\\\\\\hat f = M_G f_{\\\\\\\\text{true}} + I_G(f_{\\\\\\\\text{true}}|_{\\\\\\\\partial G})$$\\\\n    62\\\\t\\\\n    63\\\\twhere $M_G = I - \\\\\\\\chi_G$ is the multiplication mask in input space, and the second term uses the truth at the boundary because the training data immediately outside $G$ provides that information.\\\\n    64\\\\t\\\\n    65\\\\tThe **structural phantom** at any query is:\\\\n    66\\\\t\\\\n    67\\\\t$$\\\\\\\\text{phantom}_Q = P_Q f_{\\\\\\\\text{true}} - P_Q \\\\\\\\hat f = P_Q (\\\\\\\\chi_G f_{\\\\\\\\text{true}} - I_G(f_{\\\\\\\\text{true}}|_{\\\\\\\\partial G})).$$\\\\n    68\\\\t\\\\n    69\\\\tThe first term is the truth's content inside $G$ that the model never saw. The second term is the architecture's interpolation, which approximates the missing content using only the boundary. If the missing content is determined by the boundary (interpolation works), the phantom is small. If the missing content is independent of the boundary (the truth has structure in $G$ that has no surface signature on $\\\\\\\\partial G$), the phantom is large.\\\\n    70\\\\t\\\\n    71\\\\tThis is the spatial-gap phantom $w_G$ from the multiplication-defect suite, with $G$ relocated from physical space to input space. The operator-theoretic spine carries directly \\\\u2014 within the linearization assumptions stated above.\\\\n    72\\\\t\\\\n    73\\\\t### 1.3 Two Failure Modes\\\\n    74\\\\t\\\\n    75\\\\tThe training process can converge in two distinct regimes when $G$ is non-trivial:\\\\n    76\\\\t\\\\n    77\\\\t**Mode 1: Confident interpolation.** The architecture's inductive bias produces a confident extrapolation into $G$, with the model treating the hole as just another part of input space and the training process producing outputs of comparable confidence everywhere. The interpolation $I_G(f_{\\\\\\\\text{true}}|_{\\\\\\\\partial G})$ is what the model actually outputs in $G$, and its difference from $f_{\\\\\\\\text{true}}|_G$ is the phantom. This is the dominant failure mode for unconstrained training of standard architectures on selectively-filtered data \\\\u2014 the model doesn't know it doesn't know.\\\\n    78\\\\t\\\\n    79\\\\t**Mode 2: Calibrated uncertainty.** The architecture is trained or augmented to recognize regions of low data support and produce wider response distributions in $G$ than in $\\\\\\\\Omega \\\\\\\\setminus G$. The phantom shifts from first-moment (wrong content) to second-moment (inflated uncertainty). The model still doesn't have access to the truth in $G$, but it correctly signals that it doesn't.\\\\n    80\\\\t\\\\n[384 lines total]\\\",\\n2006:      \\\"json_block\\\": \\\"{\\\\\\\"language\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"markdown\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"code\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"# Training Corpus Holes as Multiplication Defects\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n## Phantom-framework formalization of selective data absence, in the linearized regime\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n*Working Document \\\\\\\\u2014 First Pass \\\\\\\\u00b7 April 2026*\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n*Companion to: Training Distortion as Forced Constraint Projection; Statistical Phantoms; Phantom Eigenmode Fabrication from Measurement Gaps; Compression\\\\\\\\u2013Commutator Geometry (v3); Information-Geometric Reformulation (nonlinear-manifold treatment)*\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n---\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n## Preface\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThis document characterizes phantom phenomenology in the **linearized regime** of trained predictors when the training corpus has selective data absence \\\\\\\\u2014 regions of input space from which the training process never receives signal, either because the data are missing or because curation has selectively excluded them.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n**The structural claim.** Data holes are a direct realization of the multiplication-defect case from the phantom suite, with the gap region $G$ relocated from physical space (the spatial-gap setting) to input space. The bulk-edge-projector three-part anatomy applies; T6-analog boundary-layer phenomena exist with the architecture's effective capacity playing the role of bandwidth; the four-object scalar decomposition (retention, suppression, leakage, total error) gives exact identities in the Hilbert setting.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n**Two separate linearizations appear in the analysis.** Both must be made explicit so the regime of validity is clear:\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n1. **The function space.** For regression-type readouts $f(x) \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\in \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{R}^d$, the predictor space is $L^2(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu; \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{R}^d)$ directly. For distributional outputs (next-token policies $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\cdot|x)$), the natural ambient space is the manifold of conditional distributions with Fisher / KL Riemannian structure \\\\\\\\u2014 a curved manifold, not a Hilbert space. The Halmos toolkit applies in the Fisher-tangent linearization at a chosen base point, with corrections governed by the second-order term $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\frac{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\lambda^2}{2}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\tilde r^2 - \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{E}_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0}[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\tilde r^2])$ in the e-projection expansion (companion `info_geometric_reformulation.md` \\\\\\\\u00a7B.5).\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n2. **The interpolation operator.** The architecture's $I_G$ \\\\\\\\u2014 the map from boundary information at $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\partial G$ to function values inside the hole \\\\\\\\u2014 is generally nonlinear. The spectral framework's machinery applies when $I_G$ is approximately linear in the local representational neighborhood of the trained model. For transformer architectures this approximation is reasonable for queries close to $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\partial G$ in the architecture's feature metric; it degrades for queries deep inside $G$.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe scope of this document is exactly the regime where both linearizations hold: distributional-output predictions in the Fisher tangent at a base point, and interpolation-operator predictions in the local feature-metric neighborhood of $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\partial G$. Within that regime, the four-object scalar decomposition is exact, the bulk-edge-projector anatomy applies, and the T6 analog has a precise (though architecture-specific) form.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nWhat lives outside this document. The strong-deviation regime \\\\\\\\u2014 where the trained policy is far from the reference, or where the query sits deep inside $G$ relative to the architecture's effective length scale \\\\\\\\u2014 requires nonlinear-manifold treatment. The companion `info_geometric_reformulation.md` carries out the parallel KL-geometric development for the constraint side; the analogous nonlinear treatment for the interpolation side (architecture-specific, with the second-order corrections to $I_G$ propagated through) is open work. Pointers to where the companion's results modify the linearized predictions are given at the relevant sections (\\\\\\\\u00a73.2, \\\\\\\\u00a75.2, \\\\\\\\u00a76.1, \\\\\\\\u00a77.1).\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n**The combined case** \\\\\\\\u2014 pre-filtered corpus plus constrained fine-tuning \\\\\\\\u2014 is the realistic deployment configuration. Earlier drafts of this document (and the trilogy's framing more generally) claimed that filter and constraint compound rather than cancel because they are shaped by the same content goals. **The companion's verified numerical work shows the opposite holds in the global KL geometry: aligned filter+constraint configurations produce the smallest total error in 211 of 240 cases (mean and median across the test family).** Section 5.2 has been rewritten accordingly. The compounding intuition was a linearized-regime artifact; the corrected statement is that aligned configurations are typically least-compounding, with anti-aligned configurations producing the strongest compounding effects. This corrects the most consequential single claim of earlier drafts.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nWhat this document does not do. The framework does not characterize the architecture's inductive bias from first principles, does not prescribe what filters do or do not damage which query distributions, does not predict specific outputs of specific systems. It identifies what kind of distortion data holes geometrically force in the linearized regime and how that distortion combines with constraint distortion when both are applied, with the corrected combining rule sourced from the companion document.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n---\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n## Part I \\\\\\\\u2014 Setup\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n### 1.1 The Input Space and the Data Distribution\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nLet $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}$ be the input space (token sequences for language models, latent codes for image generators, or whatever the model's input domain is). Let $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu$ be the data-generating distribution on $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}$. Let $f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}}: \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X} \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\to \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{Y}$ be the truth \\\\\\\\u2014 the ideal predictor the data are sampled from, in the regression-type framing, or the conditional log-density in the distributional framing.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nA **data hole** is a region $G \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\subset \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}$ where the training corpus contains no samples from $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu$. The hole may arise from genuine data absence (gaps in observation, undersampled regions) or from purposeful exclusion (curation, content filtering, demographic-balance sampling). The operator-theoretic structure does not distinguish these \\\\\\\\u2014 what matters is that the training signal in $G$ is zero.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe **filtered distribution** seen by the training process is $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\cdot \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{1}_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Omega \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\setminus G}$, where $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Omega = \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}$ is the full input space. The data the model fits is samples from this filtered distribution, not from $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu$.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n### 1.2 The Trained Predictor\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nLet $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\hat f$ denote the predictor the training process converges to. In the well-specified-model limit (architecture can represent $f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}}$, infinite capacity, infinite data on $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Omega \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\setminus G$), the trained predictor satisfies:\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n$$\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\hat f|_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Omega \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\setminus G} = f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}}|_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Omega \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\setminus G}$$\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\u2014 the trained predictor agrees with the truth on the support of the filtered distribution.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nOn $G$, the trained predictor is not determined by the data. It is determined by the architecture's inductive bias plus whatever boundary information from $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Omega \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\setminus G$ the architecture propagates. Write:\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n$$\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\hat f|_G = I_G(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\hat f|_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\partial G})$$\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nwhere $I_G$ is the **interpolation operator** of the architecture \\\\\\\\u2014 the map from boundary information at $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\partial G$ to the function value inside the hole. This is the analog of the Green's function for an elliptic operator: a way of extending boundary data into the interior. Different architectures have different $I_G$. Transformer architectures have $I_G$ that is approximately linear in the local representational neighborhood plus nonlinear corrections; classical kernel methods have $I_G$ that is exactly the kernel-weighted extension; Gaussian processes have $I_G$ that is the conditional-mean extension.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n**Linearization assumption.** The operator-theoretic spine of this document treats $I_G$ as approximately linear in the local representational neighborhood of $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\partial G$. For Gaussian processes and kernel methods this is exact. For transformer architectures it holds for queries close to $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\partial G$ in the architecture's feature metric; queries deep inside $G$ relative to the architecture's effective length scale (\\\\\\\\u00a73.2) lie outside this approximation and require nonlinear treatment that this document does not provide. The regime of validity for the predictions below is the boundary-layer neighborhood \\\\\\\\u2014 close enough to $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\partial G$ that the architecture's interpolation is well-approximated by its linear part.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe full trained predictor in the linearized regime is:\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n$$\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\hat f = M_G f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}} + I_G(f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}}|_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\partial G})$$\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nwhere $M_G = I - \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\chi_G$ is the multiplication mask in input space, and the second term uses the truth at the boundary because the training data immediately outside $G$ provides that information.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe **structural phantom** at any query is:\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n$$\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{phantom}_Q = P_Q f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}} - P_Q \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\hat f = P_Q (\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\chi_G f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}} - I_G(f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}}|_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\partial G})).$$\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe first term is the truth's content inside $G$ that the model never saw. The second term is the architecture's interpolation, which approximates the missing content using only the boundary. If the missing content is determined by the boundary (interpolation works), the phantom is small. If the missing content is independent of the boundary (the truth has structure in $G$ that has no surface signature on $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\partial G$), the phantom is large.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThis is the spatial-gap phantom $w_G$ from the multiplication-defect suite, with $G$ relocated from physical space to input space. The operator-theoretic spine carries directly \\\\\\\\u2014 within the linearization assumptions stated above.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n### 1.3 Two Failure Modes\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe training process can converge in two distinct regimes when $G$ is non-trivial:\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n**Mode 1: Confident interpolation.** The architecture's inductive bias produces a confident extrapolation into $G$, with the model treating the hole as just another part of input space and the training process producing outputs of comparable confidence everywhere. The interpolation $I_G(f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}}|_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\partial G})$ is what the model actually outputs in $G$, and its difference from $f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{true}}|_G$ is the phantom. This is the dominant failure mode for unconstrained training of standard architectures on selectively-filtered data \\\\\\\\u2014 the model doesn't know it doesn't know.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n**Mode 2: Calibrated uncertainty.** The architecture is trained or augmented to recognize regions of low data support and produce wider response distributions in $G$ than in $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Omega \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\setminus G$. The phantom shifts from first-moment (wrong content) to second-moment (inflated uncertainty). The model still doesn't have access to the truth in $G$, but it correctly signals that it doesn't.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"filename\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"/home/claude/ml/ML/training_corpus_holes.md\\\\\\\"}\\\"\\n2048:        \\\"text\\\": \\\"     1\\\\t# The Audit-Blind Subspace\\\\n     2\\\\t\\\\n     3\\\\t## An information-theoretic limit on audits of trained networks\\\\n     4\\\\t\\\\n     5\\\\t*Working Paper Draft \\\\u00b7 April 2026*\\\\n     6\\\\t\\\\n     7\\\\t*Companion to: Statistical Phantoms; Training Distortion as Forced Constraint Projection; Training Corpus Holes as Multiplication Defects; Information-Geometric Reformulation; Compression\\\\u2013Commutator Geometry (v3); Phantom Eigenmode Fabrication from Measurement Gaps*\\\\n     8\\\\t\\\\n     9\\\\t---\\\\n    10\\\\t\\\\n    11\\\\t## Abstract\\\\n    12\\\\t\\\\n    13\\\\tLet $F: \\\\\\\\Xi \\\\\\\\to H$ be the map taking a training-data or training-objective distortion $\\\\\\\\xi$ to the resulting trained-network function $f_{\\\\\\\\theta(\\\\\\\\xi)}$ in a Hilbert function space $H$. Let $\\\\\\\\mathcal{Q} = \\\\\\\\{q_1, \\\\\\\\ldots, q_N\\\\\\\\}$ be a finite collection of continuous linear functionals on $H$ \\\\u2014 an audit query suite. This limit is independent of how thoroughly the audit examines its own queries.\\\\n    30\\\\t\\\\n    31\\\\t---\\\\n    32\\\\t\\\\n    33\\\\t## Preface\\\\n    34\\\\t\\\\n    35\\\\tThis document derives the audit-blindness lower bound implicit in the framework developed across `statistical_phantoms.md`, `training_distortion_phantom.md`, and `training_corpus_holes.md`. The result is a consequence of the existing apparatus rather than a new development; the mathematics is linear algebra in the linearized regime, with the four-object decomposition from `statistical_phantoms.md` \\\\u00a71.3 carrying through directly.\\\\n    36\\\\t\\\\n    37\\\\tThe applied setting is alignment auditing: an auditor wishes to determine whether a trained network has been subjected to specific kinds of distortion during training, using only queries to the trained network. The framework's contribution is the formal observation that any such audit has an information-theoretic ceiling set by the audit suite's query coverage relative to the distortion space, and that ceiling is computable from architectural and training-procedure information alone.\\\\n    38\\\\t\\\\n    39\\\\tScope. The results below are stated and proved in the linearized regime \\\\u2014 small distortions, NTK-style local linearization for wide networks, Fisher-tangent linearization for distributional outputs. Extensions to nonlinear regimes are sketched in Part VII; quantitative magnitude corrections from curvature are governed by the second-order term in the e-projection expansion (companion `info_geometric_reformulation.md` \\\\u00a7B.5). The structural lower-bound theorems (A, B, D) are robust under the linearization; the visibility-fraction theorem (C) depends on the four-object decomposition's exactness in the Hilbert setting.\\\\n    40\\\\t\\\\n    41\\\\tOrganization. Part VII addresses extensions to white-box and procedural audit settings and to the nonlinear regime. Part VIII situates the result relative to existing literature on backdoor attacks, influence functions, and mechanistic interpretability.\\\\n    42\\\\t\\\\n    43\\\\t---\\\\n    44\\\\t\\\\n    45\\\\t## Part I \\\\u2014 Setup\\\\n    46\\\\t\\\\n    47\\\\t### 1.1 Architecture and training procedure\\\\n    48\\\\t\\\\n    49\\\\tFix:\\\\n    50\\\\t\\\\n    51\\\\t- A measurable input space $\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}$ with base measure $\\\\\\\\nu$.\\\\n    52\\\\t- An output space $\\\\\\\\mathcal{Y}$ (taken throughout to be a Euclidean space; the distributional case is reduced to the Hilbert setting in \\\\u00a71.2).\\\\n    53\\\\t- A parametric family $f: \\\\\\\\Theta \\\\\\\\to \\\\\\\\mathcal{F}(\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}, \\\\\\\\mathcal{Y})$ \\\\u2014 the **architecture** \\\\u2014 assigning to each parameter vector $\\\\\\\\theta \\\\\\\\in \\\\\\\\Theta = \\\\\\\\mathbb{R}^P$ a function $f_\\\\\\\\theta: \\\\\\\\mathcal{X} \\\\\\\\to \\\\\\\\mathcal{Y}$.\\\\n    54\\\\t- A training procedure $T$ \\\\u2014 a map from training data and objective to trained parameters $\\\\\\\\theta(\\\\\\\\cdot) : \\\\\\\\mathcal{D} \\\\\\\\to \\\\\\\\Theta$, where $\\\\\\\\mathcal{D}$ is the space of training inputs (data, reward signal, hyperparameters, init seed).\\\\n    55\\\\t\\\\n    56\\\\tThe training procedure is taken as fixed: same loss, same optimizer, same schedule, same initialization. Following the convention established in `training_distortion_phantom.md` \\\\u00a71.1, work in the **Fisher-tangent linearization** at a chosen base point $\\\\\\\\pi_0$: identify a policy $\\\\\\\\pi$ near $\\\\\\\\pi_0$ with its score $u = \\\\\\\\log \\\\\\\\pi - \\\\\\\\log \\\\\\\\pi_0$ in the tangent space $T_{\\\\\\\\pi_0}\\\\\\\\mathcal{P}$, equipped with the Fisher inner product\\\\n    69\\\\t\\\\n    70\\\\t$$\\\\\\\\langle u, v \\\\\\\\rangle_F = \\\\\\\\mathbb{E}_{x \\\\\\\\sim \\\\\\\\nu}\\\\\\\\,\\\\\\\\mathbb{E}_{y \\\\\\\\sim \\\\\\\\pi_0(\\\\\\\\cdot|x)}[u(x,y) \\\\\\\\, v(x,y)].$$\\\\n    71\\\\t\\\\n    72\\\\tIn this tangent Hilbert space the linear-algebra apparatus applies, with second-order curvature corrections governed by the e-projection expansion at the base point. All results below should be read in $H$ either as the regression-case $L^2$ space or as the Fisher tangent at a chosen base point; both are Hilbert spaces and the operator-theoretic statements are identical in form.\\\\n    73\\\\t\\\\n    74\\\\t### 1.3 The distortion space\\\\n    75\\\\t\\\\n    76\\\\tThe **distortion space** $\\\\\\\\Xi$ is a Hilbert space parametrizing modifications to the training procedure. Two examples:\\\\n    77\\\\t\\\\n    78\\\\t**Data distortion.** $\\\\\\\\Xi = L^2(\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}, \\\\\\\\nu)$ \\\\u2014 perturbations to the training data distribution density. A distortion $\\\\\\\\xi \\\\\\\\in \\\\\\\\Xi$ corresponds to training under data drawn from the perturbed distribution $\\\\\\\\nu \\\\\\\\cdot (1 + \\\\\\\\xi)$ (with $\\\\\\\\xi$ small enough to keep the distribution non-negative). This is the setting of `training_corpus_holes.md`.\\\\n    79\\\\t\\\\n    80\\\\t**Objective distortion.** $\\\\\\\\Xi = H$ \\\\u2014 perturbations to a reward function or training objective. A distortion $\\\\\\\\xi$ corresponds to training under objective $r + \\\\\\\\xi$ where $r$ is the base reward. This is closer to `training_distortion_phantom.md`.\\\\n    81\\\\t\\\\n    82\\\\t**Combined distortion.** $\\\\\\\\Xi = L^2(\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}, \\\\\\\\nu) \\\\\\\\oplus H$ \\\\u2014 both data and objective perturbations together. This is the realistic deployment setting from `training_corpus_holes.md` Part V.\\\\n    83\\\\t\\\\n    84\\\\tThe specific structure of $\\\\\\\\Xi$ does not affect the audit-blindness theorems below; what matters is that $\\\\\\\\Xi$ is a Hilbert space and the distortion-to-function map below is well-defined.\\\\n    85\\\\t\\\\n    86\\\\t### 1.4 The distortion-to-function map\\\\n    87\\\\t\\\\n    88\\\\tFix the base distortion $\\\\\\\\xi_0 = 0$ (no distortion). Under $\\\\\\\\xi$, the trained parameters become $\\\\\\\\theta(\\\\\\\\xi)$ and the trained function is $f_{\\\\\\\\theta(\\\\\\\\xi)} \\\\\\\\in H$. Define the **distortion-to-function map**\\\\n    89\\\\t\\\\n    90\\\\t$$F : \\\\\\\\Xi \\\\\\\\to H, \\\\\\\\qquad F(\\\\\\\\xi) = f_{\\\\\\\\theta(\\\\\\\\xi)}.$$\\\\n[640 lines total]\\\",\\n2062:      \\\"json_block\\\": \\\"{\\\\\\\"language\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"markdown\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"code\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"# The Audit-Blind Subspace\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n## An information-theoretic limit on audits of trained networks\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n*Working Paper Draft \\\\\\\\u00b7 April 2026*\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n*Companion to: Statistical Phantoms; Training Distortion as Forced Constraint Projection; Training Corpus Holes as Multiplication Defects; Information-Geometric Reformulation; Compression\\\\\\\\u2013Commutator Geometry (v3); Phantom Eigenmode Fabrication from Measurement Gaps*\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n---\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n## Abstract\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nLet $F: \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Xi \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\to H$ be the map taking a training-data or training-objective distortion $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\xi$ to the resulting trained-network function $f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\theta(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\xi)}$ in a Hilbert function space $H$. The **audit-blind subspace** is\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n$$\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{N}_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{audit}} := \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\ker(A_\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{Q} \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\circ dF|_0) \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\subset \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Xi,$$\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nthe set of distortion directions to which the audit map $A_\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{Q} = (q_i)_{i=1}^N$ is insensitive at first order. This limit is independent of how thoroughly the audit examines its own queries.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n---\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n## Preface\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThis document derives the audit-blindness lower bound implicit in the framework developed across `statistical_phantoms.md`, `training_distortion_phantom.md`, and `training_corpus_holes.md`. The result is a consequence of the existing apparatus rather than a new development; the mathematics is linear algebra in the linearized regime, with the four-object decomposition from `statistical_phantoms.md` \\\\\\\\u00a71.3 carrying through directly.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe applied setting is alignment auditing: an auditor wishes to determine whether a trained network has been subjected to specific kinds of distortion during training, using only queries to the trained network. The framework's contribution is the formal observation that any such audit has an information-theoretic ceiling set by the audit suite's query coverage relative to the distortion space, and that ceiling is computable from architectural and training-procedure information alone.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nScope. The results below are stated and proved in the linearized regime \\\\\\\\u2014 small distortions, NTK-style local linearization for wide networks, Fisher-tangent linearization for distributional outputs. Extensions to nonlinear regimes are sketched in Part VII; quantitative magnitude corrections from curvature are governed by the second-order term in the e-projection expansion (companion `info_geometric_reformulation.md` \\\\\\\\u00a7B.5). The structural lower-bound theorems (A, B, D) are robust under the linearization; the visibility-fraction theorem (C) depends on the four-object decomposition's exactness in the Hilbert setting.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nOrganization. Part I sets up the function space, distortion space, and linearization. Part II defines the audit map. Part III proves Theorems A\\\\\\\\u2013C on the audit-blind subspace. Part VI proves Theorem D on optimal audit construction and develops the audit-protocol-design implications. Part VII addresses extensions to white-box and procedural audit settings and to the nonlinear regime. Part VIII situates the result relative to existing literature on backdoor attacks, influence functions, and mechanistic interpretability.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n---\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n## Part I \\\\\\\\u2014 Setup\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n### 1.1 Architecture and training procedure\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nFix:\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n- A measurable input space $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}$ with base measure $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu$.\\\\\\\\n- An output space $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{Y}$ (taken throughout to be a Euclidean space; the distributional case is reduced to the Hilbert setting in \\\\\\\\u00a71.2).\\\\\\\\n- A parametric family $f: \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Theta \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\to \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{F}(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{Y})$ \\\\\\\\u2014 the **architecture** \\\\\\\\u2014 assigning to each parameter vector $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\theta \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\in \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Theta = \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{R}^P$ a function $f_\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\theta: \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X} \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\to \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{Y}$.\\\\\\\\n- A training procedure $T$ \\\\\\\\u2014 a map from training data and objective to trained parameters $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\theta(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\cdot) : \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{D} \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\to \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Theta$, where $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{D}$ is the space of training inputs (data, reward signal, hyperparameters, init seed).\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe training procedure is taken as fixed: same loss, same optimizer, same schedule, same initialization. What varies is the data and objective \\\\\\\\u2014 captured by the distortion space $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Xi$ below.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n### 1.2 The function space\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe **predictor space** is\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n$$H = L^2(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu; \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{Y})$$\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nwith inner product $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\langle f, g \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\rangle = \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\int_\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X} f(x) \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\cdot g(x) \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\, d\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu(x)$. This is a separable Hilbert space.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nFor regression-type readouts (deterministic $f_\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\theta: \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X} \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\to \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{Y}$), $f_\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\theta \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\in H$ directly, and the operator-theoretic apparatus applies without further linearization.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nFor distributional outputs (next-token policies $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\theta(y|x)$ on a discrete vocabulary), the natural ambient space is the manifold of conditional distributions with Fisher / KL Riemannian structure \\\\\\\\u2014 a curved manifold, not Hilbert. Following the convention established in `training_distortion_phantom.md` \\\\\\\\u00a71.1, work in the **Fisher-tangent linearization** at a chosen base point $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0$: identify a policy $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi$ near $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0$ with its score $u = \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\log \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi - \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\log \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0$ in the tangent space $T_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{P}$, equipped with the Fisher inner product\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n$$\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\langle u, v \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\rangle_F = \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{E}_{x \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\sim \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\,\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{E}_{y \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\sim \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\pi_0(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\cdot|x)}[u(x,y) \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\, v(x,y)].$$\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nIn this tangent Hilbert space the linear-algebra apparatus applies, with second-order curvature corrections governed by the e-projection expansion at the base point. All results below should be read in $H$ either as the regression-case $L^2$ space or as the Fisher tangent at a chosen base point; both are Hilbert spaces and the operator-theoretic statements are identical in form.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n### 1.3 The distortion space\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe **distortion space** $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Xi$ is a Hilbert space parametrizing modifications to the training procedure. Two examples:\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n**Data distortion.** $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Xi = L^2(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu)$ \\\\\\\\u2014 perturbations to the training data distribution density. A distortion $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\xi \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\in \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Xi$ corresponds to training under data drawn from the perturbed distribution $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\cdot (1 + \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\xi)$ (with $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\xi$ small enough to keep the distribution non-negative). This is the setting of `training_corpus_holes.md`.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n**Objective distortion.** $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Xi = H$ \\\\\\\\u2014 perturbations to a reward function or training objective. A distortion $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\xi$ corresponds to training under objective $r + \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\xi$ where $r$ is the base reward. This is closer to `training_distortion_phantom.md`.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n**Combined distortion.** $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Xi = L^2(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathcal{X}, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nu) \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\oplus H$ \\\\\\\\u2014 both data and objective perturbations together. This is the realistic deployment setting from `training_corpus_holes.md` Part V.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe specific structure of $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Xi$ does not affect the audit-blindness theorems below; what matters is that $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Xi$ is a Hilbert space and the distortion-to-function map below is well-defined.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n### 1.4 The distortion-to-function map\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nFix the base distortion $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\xi_0 = 0$ (no distortion). Under $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\xi$, the trained parameters become $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\theta(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\xi)$ and the trained function is $f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\theta(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\xi)} \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\in H$. Define the **distortion-to-function map**\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n$$F : \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Xi \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\to H, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\qquad F(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\xi) = f_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\theta(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\xi)}.$$\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"filename\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"/home/claude/ml/ML/audit_blind_subspace.md\\\\\\\"}\\\"\\n2152:        \\\"text\\\": \\\"{\\\\\\\"returncode\\\\\\\":0,\\\\\\\"stdout\\\\\\\":\\\\\\\"Phantom Framework \\\\u2014 ML Path Forward\\\\\\\\nFrom Static Operator Algebra to Dynamic Distortion Analysis\\\\\\\\nWorking document \\\\u2014 April 2026\\\\\\\\nThe goal is truth, not credit.\\\\\\\\n1. it's the explicit ML port of Phantom, named \\\\\\\"Phantom Framework\\\\\\\" throughout. The connection is much stronger than the PseudoSpectral one. Some of it is verbatim algebra; some of it is new theorems built on the Phantom spine; one piece is a substantive upgrade that Phantom didn't have.\\\\n\\\\n**What carries over verbatim.** The mask $M_G = I - \\\\\\\\chi_G$ in `training_corpus_holes.md` is literally the multiplication operator from `hole_problem.ipynb`, with $G$ relocated from physical space (the rounded-rectangle gap) to input space (the region of token-sequence space the training corpus never sees). Same operator, same projection algebra, same identities. The \\\\\\\"four-object decomposition\\\\\\\" $\\\\\\\\beta^2, (1-\\\\\\\\beta)^2, \\\\\\\\beta(1-\\\\\\\\beta), 1-\\\\\\\\beta$ is the discrete-projection realization of Phantom's $a_n, 1-a_n, a_n(1-a_n)$ energy law \\\\u2014 same Bernoulli structure $\\\\\\\\beta(1-\\\\\\\\beta)$, same proof from idempotency. I just ran `four_way_decomposition.py` and `test_bridge_B.py` and the algebraic identity holds to $\\\\\\\\sim 10^{-15}$, which is the projection identity the spectral suite has been verifying all along, in a different costume. The three-flavor classification (first-moment / second-moment / eigenstructure phantoms), the bulk-edge-projector anatomy, and the regime analysis all come over intact. Mitigation maps cleanly: known mask \\\\u2192 audit with full info, design measurement \\\\u2192 design audit query suite, multi-gap synthesis \\\\u2192 multi-pipeline cross-checking.\\\\n\\\\n**What's genuinely new in the ML project.** Three things go beyond Phantom:\\\\n\\\\nThe **audit-blind subspace theorem**. $\\\\\\\\dim(\\\\\\\\mathcal{N}_{\\\\\\\\text{audit}}) \\\\\\\\geq \\\\\\\\dim(\\\\\\\\Xi) - N$ for any $N$-query audit. This is the operational version of Phantom's claim that the mask creates an unobservable subspace, but stated as a quantitative dimension formula with an explicit SVD construction for optimal audit design (Theorem D). Phantom said \\\\\\\"the gap hides things\\\\\\\"; this says \\\\\\\"for $N$ queries against a $D$-dimensional distortion space, at least $D-N$ dimensions are invisible no matter how clever the audit.\\\\\\\" That's a real theorem the spectral suite didn't have.\\\\n\\\\nThe **information-geometric reformulation**. This is the substantive upgrade. The original Phantom math is Hilbert-space ($L^2$, orthogonal projections) \\\\u2014 fine for measurement gaps in spectral data, wrong as a global structure for trained policies, which live on a curved manifold of conditional distributions with KL geometry. The reformulation replaces $f_{\\\\\\\\text{trained}} = P_C f_{\\\\\\\\text{true}}$ (linear projection) with the e-projection identity $\\\\\\\\pi^* \\\\\\\\propto \\\\\\\\pi_{\\\\\\\\text{ref}} e^{\\\\\\\\lambda^* r}$ (exponential tilt), and replaces orthogonal-projection Pythagorean with the Bregman triangle including the cross-term $\\\\\\\\lambda^*(R_{\\\\\\\\text{true}} - R_0)$. This actually fixes one of the framing concerns I'd flag in Phantom: the linearization is no longer hidden, it's explicit, and the second-order curvature term $\\\\\\\\frac{\\\\\\\\lambda^2}{2}\\\\\\\\pi_0(\\\\\\\\tilde r^2 - \\\\\\\\mathbb{E}_{\\\\\\\\pi_0}[\\\\\\\\tilde r^2])$ is identified and verified. The 15\\\\u201325% slope errors in `test_bridge_B.py` Test 4 quantify exactly how much the linearization buys you and how much it costs.\\\\n\\\\nThe **natural-vs-induced hole distinction** in `Phantom_ML_PathForward.docx`. Natural hole: data was never there, model is constructing in $G$. Induced hole: data *was* there, training suppressed it, parameters carry memory of what was suppressed. This distinction has no analog in spectral Phantom (a measurement gap is just a gap \\\\u2014 there's no \\\\\\\"the gap used to be filled and got erased\\\\\\\"). It's an ML-native concept and it weakens the audit-blind theorem in a specific way the docx works through: induced suppression leaves traces that surface probes miss, so $N$ queries plus $N$ random probe directions don't actually catch you up to dimension $N$.\\\\n\\\\n**Connection to PseudoSpectral is thin.** The only real bridge is NTK as a linearization regime \\\\u2014 `ml_path_forward.md` Phase 1 calls out wide networks in the Neural Tangent Kernel limit as where the audit-blind theorems become exact, which is methodologically the same move as PseudoSpectral Part 4's adjoint linearization. But PseudoSpectral's heavy machinery (Chebyshev pseudospectral diff, Clenshaw\\\\u2013Curtis, ETDRK4, KKT/SNOPT/IPOPT) has nothing to do with ML's numerics, which are random projections and simplex computations. The \\\\\\\"pseudospectra\\\\\\\" of Part 14 don't appear here.\\\\n\\\\n**Same author signature, same self-correction discipline.** Eleven explicit retraction rounds in the GeometricML README, with the most distinctive claim (Bridge B as a \\\\\\\"derivation with $r \\\\\\\\approx 0.996$\\\\\\\") walked back to \\\\\\\"reorganization with 15\\\\u201325% magnitude error.\\\\\\\" The retraction list mirrors the Phantom T6 eight-errors postmortem in tone exactly: structural mistakes named, why-it-was-wrong stated, what-the-correct-version-is given. Both projects retract the same kind of thing \\\\u2014 overstated universality claims, under-qualified compounding intuitions, regime-of-validity slippage.\\\\n\\\\n**Where this framework actually stands.** It's farther from the spectral version than the README suggests. Phantom's `numerics.ipynb` shows the math doing what the theory says on real domains; the ML side has the algebraic identities verified to machine precision and Bridge B verified at $r \\\\\\\\approx 0.98$ on toy projections, but the README explicitly flags that translating to production RLHF has unaddressed obstacles: real systems use learned reward models with many effective directions (not a single linear $r$), the loss-function KL coefficient \\\\u2260 the m-flat constraint severity except at the optimum, \\\\\\\"off-query behavior\\\\\\\" lacks an operational definition for an LLM, and the constraint multiplier $\\\\\\\\mu$ isn't directly observable. So the toy-level results stand, the framework predictions are clear, but the contact with actual trained models is open work \\\\u2014 same gap the spectral side has between operator theory and physical experiments, just larger because the production object (a trained transformer) is messier than a Bessel-mode Chladni plate.\\\",\\n2657:        \\\"text\\\": \\\"     1\\\\t# Statistical Phantoms\\\\n     2\\\\t\\\\n     3\\\\t## Operator geometry of measurement defects in downstream inference\\\\n     4\\\\t\\\\n     5\\\\t*Working Document \\\\u2014 First Development Pass \\\\u00b7 April 2026*\\\\n     6\\\\t\\\\n     7\\\\t*Companion to: Phantom Eigenmode Fabrication from Measurement Gaps; Compression\\\\u2013Commutator Geometry (v3); Correlation and Matched Filtering Under a Common Measurement Gap*\\\\n     8\\\\t\\\\n     9\\\\t---\\\\n    10\\\\t\\\\n    11\\\\t## Preface\\\\n    12\\\\t\\\\n    13\\\\tThis document extends the phantom framework from spatial measurement gaps to a broader class of inference defects that share the same operator-theoretic skeleton. The claim is that phantom phenomenology \\\\u2014 false content at modes/coefficients/directions where the source has no energy, with the false content set entirely by the geometry of two non-commuting objects \\\\u2014 is a property of any inference pipeline where the inference projection and the measurement projection do not commute. Spatial gaps are one realization. Omitted variables, masked covariances, time-windowed spectra, sensor-restricted modal analysis, and several others are different realizations of the same anatomy.\\\\n    14\\\\t\\\\n    15\\\\tStatus. This is a first development pass with one subsequent correction. The abstract spine (Part I) is settled in the literature on two-projection geometry; the synthesis claim \\\\u2014 that the spine carries a phantom anatomy, the mask-adapted basis, the concentration operator, and the design rules across realizations \\\\u2014 is the document's contribution. The realizations are developed unevenly. Regression, PCA, and matched filtering are worked through; modal analysis is sketched; cross-spectral and information-theoretic cases are flagged for development. The disconfirmer protocol (Part V) is the part most likely to need revision after the realizations are fully populated.\\\\n    16\\\\t\\\\n    17\\\\t**Correction (round 11).** Earlier drafts called $\\\\\\\\beta(1-\\\\\\\\beta)$ the \\\\\\\"universal phantom energy law\\\\\\\" and treated it as the scalar magnitude for phantom phenomenology generically. This was a structural error: $\\\\\\\\beta(1-\\\\\\\\beta)$ is the **leakage** formula for one of four distinct objects (retention $\\\\\\\\beta^2$, suppression $(1-\\\\\\\\beta)^2$, leakage $\\\\\\\\beta(1-\\\\\\\\beta)$, total error $1-\\\\\\\\beta$), each with its own scalar law and peak location in $\\\\\\\\beta$. \\\\u00a71.3 has been rewritten to introduce all four; downstream sections that referenced \\\\\\\"the energy law\\\\\\\" have been updated to refer to the four-object decomposition or to the specific object being computed. The math in Parts II\\\\u2013V is mostly unaffected \\\\u2014 the three-flavor classification, the realizations in regression/PCA/matched filtering, and the disconfirmer protocols don't depend on which specific scalar law is being invoked. What changes is the framing: the synthesis is at the level of the underlying eigensystem of $T_{P_1}(P_2)$, not at the level of a single scalar formula.\\\\n    18\\\\t\\\\n    19\\\\tWhat this document is not. It is not a generalization of the multiplication-defect machinery. The locality structure of $\\\\\\\\chi_G$ \\\\u2014 boundary supports, finite-band envelopes, T6 \\\\u2014 does not lift to the abstract setting and is correctly flagged as special to that case. The gain in moving abstract is unification across statistical pipelines; the loss is the engineering specificity that makes the spatial-gap case directly designable.\\\\n    20\\\\t\\\\n    21\\\\t---\\\\n    22\\\\t\\\\n    23\\\\t## Part I \\\\u2014 The Abstract Spine: Two-Projection Geometry\\\\n    24\\\\t\\\\n    25\\\\t### 1.1 Setup\\\\n    26\\\\t\\\\n    27\\\\tLet $H$ be a separable Hilbert space and $P_1, P_2$ orthogonal projections on $H$. The pair is non-trivial if $P_1 P_2 \\\\\\\\neq P_2 P_1$. The phantom phenomenology lives entirely on $H_{\\\\\\\\mathrm{gen}}$. On $H_{\\\\\\\\mathrm{gen}}$ there is a unitary $U$ and an orthonormal basis in which:\\\\n    40\\\\t\\\\n    41\\\\t$$P_1 = U \\\\\\\\begin{pmatrix} I & 0 \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\ 0 & 0 \\\\\\\\end{pmatrix} U^*, \\\\\\\\qquad P_2 = U \\\\\\\\begin{pmatrix} C^2 & CS \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\ CS & S^2 \\\\\\\\end{pmatrix} U^*,$$\\\\n    42\\\\t\\\\n    43\\\\twith $C = \\\\\\\\mathrm{diag}(\\\\\\\\cos\\\\\\\\theta_j)$, $S = \\\\\\\\mathrm{diag}(\\\\\\\\sin\\\\\\\\theta_j)$, $0 < \\\\\\\\theta_j < \\\\\\\\pi/2$, $C^2 + S^2 = I$. This is the **canonical CS decomposition** for two projections.\\\\n    44\\\\t\\\\n    45\\\\t### 1.3 The Compression and the Four Phantom Objects\\\\n    46\\\\t\\\\n    47\\\\tDefine the **compression of $P_2$ to $\\\\\\\\mathrm{Range}(P_1)$**:\\\\n    48\\\\t\\\\n    49\\\\t$$T_{P_1}(P_2) := P_1 P_2 P_1 \\\\\\\\big|_{\\\\\\\\mathrm{Range}(P_1)}.$$\\\\n    50\\\\t\\\\n    51\\\\tOn the generic block, $T_{P_1}(P_2) = C^2$ with eigenvalues $\\\\\\\\beta_j = \\\\\\\\cos^2 \\\\\\\\theta_j \\\\\\\\in (0, 1)$. On the trivial blocks, the eigenvalues are exactly $0$ or $1$. Let $\\\\\\\\{u_j\\\\\\\\}$ be the eigenbasis of $T_{P_1}(P_2)$ on $\\\\\\\\mathrm{Range}(P_1)$, with associated canonical pairs $\\\\\\\\{v_j\\\\\\\\} \\\\\\\\subset \\\\\\\\mathrm{Range}(P_2)$ from the CS decomposition (so $P_2 u_j = \\\\\\\\cos^2\\\\\\\\theta_j \\\\\\\\cdot u_j + \\\\\\\\cos\\\\\\\\theta_j \\\\\\\\sin\\\\\\\\theta_j \\\\\\\\cdot v_j$ on the generic block).\\\\n    52\\\\t\\\\n    53\\\\tFor $f = \\\\\\\\sum_j a_j u_j \\\\\\\\in \\\\\\\\mathrm{Range}(P_1)$, the geometry of $P_2 f$ relative to $\\\\\\\\mathrm{Range}(P_1)$ produces **four scalar invariants**, each with its own norm-squared formula and its own peak location in $\\\\\\\\beta$. Direct computation in the CS basis:\\\\n    54\\\\t\\\\n    55\\\\t| Object | Norm-squared | Peaks at |\\\\n    56\\\\t|---|---|---|\\\\n    57\\\\t| **Retention** $\\\\\\\\;P_1 P_2 P_1 f$ | $\\\\\\\\sum_j |a_j|^2 \\\\\\\\beta_j^2$ | $\\\\\\\\beta = 1$ |\\\\n    58\\\\t| **Suppression** $\\\\\\\\;P_1 f - P_1 P_2 P_1 f$ | $\\\\\\\\sum_j |a_j|^2 (1 - \\\\\\\\beta_j)^2$ | $\\\\\\\\beta = 0$ |\\\\n    59\\\\t| **Leakage** $\\\\\\\\;(I - P_1) P_2 f$ | $\\\\\\\\sum_j |a_j|^2 \\\\\\\\beta_j (1 - \\\\\\\\beta_j)$ | $\\\\\\\\beta = 1/2$ |\\\\n    60\\\\t| **Total error** $\\\\\\\\;f - P_2 f$ | $\\\\\\\\sum_j |a_j|^2 (1 - \\\\\\\\beta_j)$ | $\\\\\\\\beta = 0$ |\\\\n    61\\\\t\\\\n    62\\\\tThese four are not independent. The key per-mode identity is\\\\n    63\\\\t\\\\n    64\\\\t$$1 - \\\\\\\\beta_j \\\\\\\\;=\\\\\\\\; (1 - \\\\\\\\beta_j)^2 + \\\\\\\\beta_j (1 - \\\\\\\\beta_j),$$\\\\n    65\\\\t\\\\n    66\\\\tequivalently **total error = suppression + leakage**. This is the orthogonal decomposition of $f - P_2 f$ into its $\\\\\\\\mathrm{Range}(P_1)$ component (suppression \\\\u2014 the part of $f$ that the measurement *removes* in the inference subspace) and its $\\\\\\\\mathrm{Range}(P_1)^\\\\\\\\perp$ component (leakage \\\\u2014 the part of $P_2 f$ that *escapes* the inference subspace). Retention $P_1 P_2 P_1 f$ is what survives the pass through both projections.\\\\n    67\\\\t\\\\n    68\\\\t**Leakage in particular** \\\\u2014 the off-target content $w(f) = (I - P_1) P_2 f$, the part of $P_2 f$ that lies outside $\\\\\\\\mathrm{Range}(P_1)$ \\\\u2014 has scalar law $\\\\\\\\sum_j |a_j|^2 \\\\\\\\beta_j (1 - \\\\\\\\beta_j)$, **Bernoulli variance evaluated at the cosine-squared of principal angles**. It is the same law that appears in the spatial-gap case as $a_n(1 - a_n)$ \\\\u2014 the energy that leaked from mode $n_0$ into other modes due to the mask, computed as $\\\\\\\\sum_{m \\\\\\\\neq n_0} |\\\\\\\\hat c_m|^2 = a_{n_0}(1 - a_{n_0})|c|^2$ in `common_mask_correlation` \\\\u00a72(3). The maximum of $\\\\\\\\beta(1-\\\\\\\\beta)$ at $\\\\\\\\beta = 1/2$ corresponds to principal angle $\\\\\\\\theta = \\\\\\\\pi/4$ \\\\u2014 the configuration that maximizes redistribution between the two subspaces. $\\\\\\\\beta = 0$ (orthogonal) and $\\\\\\\\beta = 1$ (coincident) both give zero leakage.\\\\n    69\\\\t\\\\n    70\\\\t**Earlier drafts of this document called $\\\\\\\\beta(1-\\\\\\\\beta)$ \\\\\\\"the universal phantom energy law\\\\\\\" and treated it as the scalar magnitude for phantom phenomenology generically.** That framing was a structural error: it conflated leakage with the other three objects, each of which has its own scalar law and its own peak location. The conflation is consequential because different downstream computations are governed by different objects:\\\\n    71\\\\t\\\\n    72\\\\t- **First-moment biases** (the regression case in \\\\u00a74.1, where the omitted-variable bias $\\\\\\\\mathbb{E}[\\\\\\\\hat\\\\\\\\beta_o] - \\\\\\\\beta_o$ is a deterministic shift) are governed by the **suppression** object. The claim is that phantom phenomenology \\\\\\\\u2014 false content at modes/coefficients/directions where the source has no energy, with the false content set entirely by the geometry of two non-commuting objects \\\\\\\\u2014 is a property of any inference pipeline where the inference projection and the measurement projection do not commute. Spatial gaps are one realization. Omitted variables, masked covariances, time-windowed spectra, sensor-restricted modal analysis, and several others are different realizations of the same anatomy.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nStatus. This is a first development pass with one subsequent correction. The abstract spine (Part I) is settled in the literature on two-projection geometry; the synthesis claim \\\\\\\\u2014 that the spine carries a phantom anatomy, the mask-adapted basis, the concentration operator, and the design rules across realizations \\\\\\\\u2014 is the document's contribution. The realizations are developed unevenly. Regression, PCA, and matched filtering are worked through; modal analysis is sketched; cross-spectral and information-theoretic cases are flagged for development. The disconfirmer protocol (Part V) is the part most likely to need revision after the realizations are fully populated.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n**Correction (round 11).** Earlier drafts called $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\beta(1-\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\beta)$ the \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"universal phantom energy law\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\" and treated it as the scalar magnitude for phantom phenomenology generically. This document parallels the PCA-with-mask companion (`pca_masked_companion.md`) in establishing that the framework's predictions for omitted-variable regression match the empirical bias structure pointwise.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe structural claim from `statistical_phantoms.md` \\\\\\\\u00a74.1: the omitted-variable bias\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n$$\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\mathbb{E}[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\hat\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\beta_o] - \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\beta_o = (X_o^T X_o)^{-1} X_o^T X_u \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\beta_u$$\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nis the regression analog of the spectral phantom $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\hat c_m = -K_{m,n_0} c$, with the **regression coupling kernel**\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n$$K_{\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\text{reg}} := (X_o^T X_o)^{-1} X_o^T X_u$$\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nplaying the role of the off-diagonal coupling matrix. The four-object decomposition (`statistical_phantoms.md` \\\\\\\\u00a71.3) applies with $P_1 = P_u$ (unobserved column space) and $P_2 = P_o$ (observed column space), with principal angles between them being the canonical correlations $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\rho_j = \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\cos\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\theta_j$ (Hotelling 1936).\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThis document:\\\\\\\\n1. The technique is foundational in structural health monitoring, civil engineering, and aerospace: monitor a structure's recovered modes over time, detect changes, flag changes as damage indicators.\\\\n    14\\\\t\\\\n    15\\\\tThe framework's claim from `statistical_phantoms.md` \\\\u00a74.5: *under sensor restriction (sub-sampling of the full DOF set), the recovered modes are not the truth restricted to sensors but eigenvectors of $M_S \\\\\\\\Sigma M_S$, where $\\\\\\\\Sigma$ is the full-DOF response covariance and $M_S$ is the sensor-mask operator*. The technique is foundational in structural health monitoring, civil engineering, and aerospace: monitor a structure's recovered modes over time, detect changes, flag changes as damage indicators.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe framework's claim from `statistical_phantoms.md` \\\\\\\\u00a74.5: *under sensor restriction (sub-sampling of the full DOF set), the recovered modes are not the truth restricted to sensors but eigenvectors of $M_S \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Sigma M_S$, where $\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\Sigma$ is the full-DOF response covariance and $M_S$ is the sensor-mask operator*. Mathematically, this is the discrete analog of the PCA-with-spatial-mask case (`pca_masked_companion.md`); the structural form is identical.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nOperationally, the consequence is striking: **changes in sensor configuration produce apparent changes in recovered modes that are not structural changes.** Damage-detection methods that flag mode-shape changes are vulnerable to false alarms when the sensor set is reconfigured (sensor failure, replacement, reinstallation after maintenance). The framework reads this as: the recovered modes are partly the structure's modes and partly the sensor configuration's signature; you cannot separate them without auxiliary information.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThis document demonstrates the framework's prediction and the damage-detection false-alarm phenomenon.
